\chapter{ggNetView manual documentation}\label{ggnetview-manual-documentation}

\section{Introdution}\label{introdution}

\textbf{Network analysis} has been widely applied in the \textbf{life sciences, microbiology, ecology, and agronomy} to quantify interactions among \textbf{genes, proteins, metabolites, microorganisms, and environmental factors}. These interactions collectively shape the functioning of biological and ecological systems. Despite its broad adoption, existing tools often face limitations in flexibility, customization, reproducibility, and the generation of publication-ready figures.

To address these challenges, \textbf{ggNetView} was developed as an \textbf{R package} that integrates \textbf{ggplot2, ggraph, and tidygraph} within the \textbf{Grammar of Graphics framework}, enabling fully reproducible and highly customizable network visualizations. The package provides deterministic layout algorithms, comprehensive topological analyses, modular coloring schemes, hierarchical annotations, and consistent theming, ensuring standardized and interpretable graphical output.

Although initially designed for applications in \textbf{soil science and microbial ecology}, \textbf{ggNetView} is broadly applicable to network analyses in \texttt{molecular\ biology}, including \textbf{WGCNA and protein--protein interaction (PPI) networks}. By lowering technical barriers in network construction and visualization, \textbf{ggNetView} enables researchers across disciplines to efficiently produce reproducible, publication-quality network figures.

\section{Installation}\label{installation}

\begin{quote}
Step1: install CRAN dependencies
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{cran\_pkgs }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}
  \StringTok{"boot"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"dplyr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"FNN"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"future"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"future.apply"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"ggforce"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"ggnewscale"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"ggplot2"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"ggraph"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"ggrepel"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"Hmisc"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"huge"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"igraph"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"magrittr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"MASS"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"Matrix"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"patchwork"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"progressr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"psych"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"purrr"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"qgraph"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Rcpp"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"RcppArmadillo"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"readr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"rlang"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"scales"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"scatterpie"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"stringr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"tibble"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"tidygraph"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"tidyr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"vegan"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"VGAM"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"WGCNA"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{new\_pkgs }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ cran\_pkgs[}\SpecialCharTok{!}\NormalTok{cran\_pkgs }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \FunctionTok{installed.packages}\NormalTok{()[, }\StringTok{"Package"}\NormalTok{]]}
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (}\FunctionTok{length}\NormalTok{(new\_pkgs)) }\FunctionTok{install.packages}\NormalTok{(new\_pkgs)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Step2: (optional) install suggested packages
\end{quote}

These packages are not required for the core functionality, but enable additional features (e.g.~dynamic tree cut, node influence, vignettes, tests):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{install.packages}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"dynamicTreeCut"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"influential"}\NormalTok{,}
                   \StringTok{"knitr"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"rmarkdown"}\NormalTok{,}
                   \StringTok{"RobustRankAggreg"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"testthat"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Step3: install ggNetView from GitHub
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# install.packages("devtools")}
\NormalTok{devtools}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{install\_github}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Jiawang1209/ggNetView"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\chapter{Build graph object}\label{build-graph-object}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

Basic workflow of \textbf{ggNetView}：

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Build a \textbf{graph object}
\item
  Understand and manipulate the \textbf{graph object}
\item
  Visualize the network using \textbf{layout algorithms}
\item
  Retrieve \textbf{topology} of network
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\begin{quote}
Load R Package
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(tidyverse)   }\CommentTok{\# data manipulation / piping utilities}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggNetView)   }\CommentTok{\# graph builders + info{-}extraction helpers}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(igraph)      }\CommentTok{\# igraph object}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Build graph from matrix}\label{build-graph-from-matrix}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Built{-}in example datasets shipped with ggNetView {-}{-}{-}{-}}

\CommentTok{\# Raw ASV/OTU abundance table (rows = ASVs/OTUs, cols = samples; integer counts).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_tab)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859   18
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{otu\_tab[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##        KO1  KO2  KO3  KO4  KO5
## ASV_1 1113 1968  816 1372 1062
## ASV_2 1922 1227 2355 2218 2885
## ASV_3  568  460  899  902 1226
## ASV_4 1433  400  535  759 1287
## ASV_6  882  673  819  888 1475
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Same table after rarefaction (sequencing depth normalised across samples).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859   18
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{otu\_rare[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##        KO1  KO2  KO3  KO4  KO5
## ASV_1  992 1636  604 1084  806
## ASV_2 1725 1018 1814 1743 2196
## ASV_3  520  389  687  701  932
## ASV_4 1280  328  425  580 1004
## ASV_6  794  557  633  706 1142
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Rarefied table converted to relative abundance — most common input for correlation networks.}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare\_relative"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859   18
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{otu\_rare\_relative[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##              KO1        KO2        KO3        KO4        KO5
## ASV_1 0.03306667 0.05453333 0.02013333 0.03613333 0.02686667
## ASV_2 0.05750000 0.03393333 0.06046667 0.05810000 0.07320000
## ASV_3 0.01733333 0.01296667 0.02290000 0.02336667 0.03106667
## ASV_4 0.04266667 0.01093333 0.01416667 0.01933333 0.03346667
## ASV_6 0.02646667 0.01856667 0.02110000 0.02353333 0.03806667
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Taxonomic annotation for the ASVs/OTUs above.}
\CommentTok{\# First column must be the ASV/OTU ID (matches rownames of the abundance tables).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"tax\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(tax\_tab)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859    8
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{tax\_tab[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tibble: 5 x 5
##   OTUID  Kingdom  Phylum          Class          Order            
##   <chr>  <chr>    <chr>           <chr>          <chr>            
## 1 ASV_2  Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 2 ASV_3  Bacteria Verrucomicrobia Spartobacteria Unassigned       
## 3 ASV_31 Bacteria Actinobacteria  Actinobacteria Actinomycetales  
## 4 ASV_27 Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 5 ASV_9  Bacteria Unassigned      Unassigned     Unassigned
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build a co{-}occurrence network directly from an abundance matrix {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Internal pipeline: transform {-}\textgreater{} correlation {-}\textgreater{} p{-}value adjustment {-}\textgreater{}}
\CommentTok{\# edge filtering {-}\textgreater{} community detection {-}\textgreater{} attach taxonomy.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,  }\CommentTok{\# variables x samples numeric matrix}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# input is already relative abundance, no extra transform}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff (subjective; consider ggNetView\_RMT)}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# adjusted p{-}value cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: WGCNA::corAndPvalue}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# Pearson correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"bonferroni"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,            }\CommentTok{\# taxonomy joined onto each node by name}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                 }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Inspect node/edge counts and the columns now attached to nodes:}
\CommentTok{\# modularity, modularity2, modularity3, Modularity, Degree, Strength + taxonomy.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tbl_graph: 213 nodes and 844 edges
## #
## # An undirected simple graph with 29 components
## #
## # Node Data: 213 x 14 (active)
##    name    modularity modularity2 modularity3 Modularity Degree Strength Kingdom
##    <chr>   <fct>      <ord>       <chr>       <ord>       <dbl>    <dbl> <chr>  
##  1 ASV_649 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  2 ASV_705 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  3 ASV_12~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  4 ASV_13~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  5 ASV_14~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  6 ASV_14~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  7 ASV_24~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  8 ASV_25~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.4 Bacter~
##  9 ASV_28~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
## 10 ASV_28~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
## # i 203 more rows
## # i 6 more variables: Phylum <chr>, Class <chr>, Order <chr>, Family <chr>,
## #   Genus <chr>, Species <chr>
## #
## # Edge Data: 844 x 5
##    from    to weight correlation corr_direction
##   <int> <int>  <dbl>       <dbl> <chr>         
## 1   194   195  0.959       0.959 Positive      
## 2   185   208  0.954       0.954 Positive      
## 3   185   213  0.957       0.957 Positive      
## # i 841 more rows
\end{verbatim}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Build graph from data frame}\label{build-graph-from-data-frame}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Edge{-}list style example data (protein{-}protein interactions).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ppi\_example"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}df\textasciigrave{}: edge list — typically 3 columns: node1 | node2 | weight (weight optional).}
\NormalTok{df }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ ppi\_example}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{ppi}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(df)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##   from  to    weight
## 1   A1 D40  9.306533
## 2   A2 D39 11.783920
## 3   A3 D38 23.005025
## 4   A4 D37  7.412060
## 5   A5 D36 18.778894
## 6   A6 D35 16.592965
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}node\_annotation\textasciigrave{}: per{-}node metadata; first column = node ID.}
\NormalTok{node\_annotation }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ ppi\_example}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{annotation}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(node\_annotation)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##   node group
## 1   A1     A
## 2   A2     A
## 3   A3     A
## 4   A4     A
## 5   A5     A
## 6   A6     A
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build a tbl\_graph directly from an edge{-}list data.frame {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# No correlation step — edges are taken as{-}is from \textasciigrave{}df\textasciigrave{}.}
\CommentTok{\# Community detection still runs on the resulting graph.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_df }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_df}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{df              =}\NormalTok{ df,                }\CommentTok{\# edge list (node1 | node2 | weight)}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation =}\NormalTok{ node\_annotation,   }\CommentTok{\# per{-}node metadata, joined by node name}
  \AttributeTok{directed        =}\NormalTok{ F,                 }\CommentTok{\# undirected network}
  \AttributeTok{module.method   =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules     =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed            =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_df}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tbl_graph: 100 nodes and 50 edges
## #
## # An unrooted forest with 50 trees
## #
## # Node Data: 100 x 9 (active)
##    name  group modularity modularity2 modularity3 Modularity Degree Segree
##    <chr> <chr> <fct>      <fct>       <chr>       <fct>       <dbl>  <dbl>
##  1 C13   C     1          1           1           1               1      1
##  2 C28   C     1          1           1           1               1      1
##  3 C2    C     10         10          10          10              1      1
##  4 D9    D     10         10          10          10              1      1
##  5 A3    A     11         11          11          11              1      1
##  6 D38   D     11         11          11          11              1      1
##  7 B12   B     12         12          12          12              1      1
##  8 D19   D     12         12          12          12              1      1
##  9 A1    A     13         13          13          13              1      1
## 10 D40   D     13         13          13          13              1      1
## # i 90 more rows
## # i 1 more variable: Strength <dbl>
## #
## # Edge Data: 50 x 4
##    from    to weight correlation
##   <int> <int>  <dbl>       <dbl>

## 2    15    16   50.6        50.6

## # i 47 more rows

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Same builder, but WITHOUT a node{-}annotation table {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Useful when only an edge list is available and no extra metadata to attach.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_df2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_df}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{df              =}\NormalTok{ df,                }\CommentTok{\# edge list}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation =} \ConstantTok{NULL}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# skip the annotation join entirely}
  \AttributeTok{directed        =}\NormalTok{ F,                 }\CommentTok{\# undirected network}
  \AttributeTok{module.method   =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules     =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed            =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_df2}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Build graph from data frame with module}\label{build-graph-from-data-frame-with-module}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Same shape as ppi\_example, but \textasciigrave{}annotation\textasciigrave{} already contains a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{}}
\CommentTok{\# column (module assignments computed elsewhere — we will NOT recompute them).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ppi\_module"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{df }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ ppi\_module}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{ppi}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(df)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##   from  to    weight
## 1   A1 D40  9.306533
## 2   A2 D39 11.783920
## 3   A3 D38 23.005025
## 4   A4 D37  7.412060
## 5   A5 D36 18.778894
## 6   A6 D35 16.592965
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{node\_annotation }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ ppi\_module}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{annotation}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(node\_annotation)   }\CommentTok{\# }\AlertTok{NOTE}\CommentTok{: must contain a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} column}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##   node Modularity
## 1   A1          A
## 2   A2          A
## 3   A3          A
## 4   A4          A
## 5   A5          A
## 6   A6          A
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} \textasciigrave{}\_module\textasciigrave{} variant: keep pre{-}computed module assignments {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Use this when you already have modules (e.g. from a previous WGCNA run,}
\CommentTok{\# expert curation, biological prior). Internal community detection is skipped.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_module }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_module}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{df              =}\NormalTok{ df,                }\CommentTok{\# edge list}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation =}\NormalTok{ node\_annotation,   }\CommentTok{\# MUST contain a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} column}
  \AttributeTok{directed        =}\NormalTok{ F,                 }\CommentTok{\# undirected network}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules     =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed            =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_from\_module}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Example data

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Square adjacency matrix (already{-}filtered weights for every node pair).}
\CommentTok{\# Useful when the network was built externally (NetCoMi, FlashWeave, ...).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"adjacency\_matrix\_example"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(adjacency\_matrix\_example)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Reuse the same taxonomy table as node annotation.}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"tax\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(tax\_tab)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

## # A tibble: 5 x 5
##   OTUID  Kingdom  Phylum          Class          Order            
##   <chr>  <chr>    <chr>           <chr>          <chr>            
## 1 ASV_2  Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 2 ASV_3  Bacteria Verrucomicrobia Spartobacteria Unassigned       
## 3 ASV_31 Bacteria Actinobacteria  Actinobacteria Actinomycetales  
## 4 ASV_27 Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 5 ASV_9  Bacteria Unassigned      Unassigned     Unassigned
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build a tbl\_graph from a pre{-}computed adjacency matrix {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Community detection runs internally because we have weights but no modules yet.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_adj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_adj\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{adjacency\_matrix =}\NormalTok{ adjacency\_matrix\_example, }\CommentTok{\# square weighted adjacency matrix}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,                  }\CommentTok{\# per{-}node metadata, joined by node name}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                       }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                      \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_adj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Build graph from adjacency matrix with module information}\label{build-graph-from-adjacency-matrix-with-module-information}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Add a pre{-}existing module assignment to the annotation table.}
\CommentTok{\# (Demo only) treat the \textasciigrave{}Kingdom\textasciigrave{} column as the module label;}
\CommentTok{\# in real use, supply your actual module assignments.}
\NormalTok{tax\_tab\_module }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
\NormalTok{  dplyr}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{Modularity =}\NormalTok{ Kingdom)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} \textasciigrave{}\_module\textasciigrave{} variant for adjacency input {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# REQUIRES \textasciigrave{}node\_annotation\textasciigrave{} to contain a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} column.}
\CommentTok{\# Internal community detection is skipped; supplied modules are used directly.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_adj\_module }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_adj\_mat\_module}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{adjacency\_matrix =}\NormalTok{ adjacency\_matrix\_example, }\CommentTok{\# square weighted adjacency matrix}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab\_module,           }\CommentTok{\# MUST contain a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} column}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                       }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                      \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 3 we use the 3  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_adj\_module}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\CommentTok{\# Combined node{-}annotation table covering BOTH feature sets above.}

\begin{verbatim}
##    name      type

## 2 BASV2 Bacterial
## 3 BASV3 Bacterial
## 4 BASV4 Bacterial
## 5 BASV5 Bacterial
## 6 BASV6 Bacterial
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Cross{-}domain (bipartite{-}style) network {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Correlations are computed BETWEEN features in mat1 and features in mat2}
\CommentTok{\# (no within{-}mat1 / within{-}mat2 edges), then thresholded, modularised,}
\CommentTok{\# and joined with the node{-}annotation table.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_double\_mat }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_double\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat1            =}\NormalTok{ BASV\_tab,            }\CommentTok{\# set A (e.g. bacteria), variables x samples}
  \AttributeTok{mat2            =}\NormalTok{ FASV\_tab,            }\CommentTok{\# set B (e.g. fungi),    variables x samples}
  \AttributeTok{module.method   =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation =}\NormalTok{ double\_mat\_node\_df,  }\CommentTok{\# node metadata covering BOTH mat1 and mat2}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules     =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                  }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed            =} \DecValTok{1115}                 \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 4 we use the 4  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{graph\_obj\_double\_mat}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Build graph from igraph object}\label{build-graph-from-igraph-object}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Load the PPI example dataset (edge list + node annotation).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ppi\_example"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Build a native igraph object directly from the edge list.}
\CommentTok{\# This is the format users coming from an igraph workflow typically already have.}
\NormalTok{igraph\_object }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ igraph}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{graph\_from\_data\_frame}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{d        =}\NormalTok{ ppi\_example}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{ppi,          }\CommentTok{\# edge list}
  \AttributeTok{vertices =}\NormalTok{ ppi\_example}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{annotation,   }\CommentTok{\# vertex metadata}
  \AttributeTok{directed =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}                     \CommentTok{\# undirected network}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{igraph\_object}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## IGRAPH 36fcfc3 UNW- 100 200 -- 
## + attr: name (v/c), group (v/c), weight (e/n)
## + edges from 36fcfc3 (vertex names):
##  [1] A1 --D40 A2 --D39 A3 --D38 A4 --D37 A5 --D36 A6 --D35 A7 --D34 A8 --D33
##  [9] A9 --D32 A10--D31 B1 --D30 B2 --D29 B3 --D28 B4 --D27 B5 --D26 B6 --D25

\section{Build graph from WGCNA}\label{build-graph-from-wgcna}

\begin{verbatim}
# ---- Importing an existing WGCNA pipeline output ----
# (Not evaluated here — requires external files saved by WGCNA::blockwiseModules.)

# 1. Load the topological overlap matrix (TOM) saved by WGCNA.
load(file = "WGCNA.tom-block.1.RData")
TOM_mat <- as.matrix(TOM)

# 2. Load the abundance matrix used to build that TOM
#    (kept for downstream re-thresholding / sanity checks).
plot.mat = readRDS(file = "plot.mat.Rds")

# 3. Sparsify the TOM: keep only the top_k strongest TOM connections per node.
trans_TOM <- trans_TOM_in_WGCNA(
  TOM_mat,     # square TOM matrix from WGCNA
  plot.mat,    # original abundance matrix the TOM came from
  top_k = 1    # keep top_k strongest neighbours per node
)

# 4. Load the WGCNA net object (contains module colour assignments).
net <- readRDS(file = "net.Rds")

# 5. Build the module table — one row per feature; columns: ID + Module.
module <- data.frame(
  ID     = names(net$colors),          # feature IDs
  Module = as.character(net$colors)    # WGCNA module colour as module label
)

# 6. Optional ordering of node IDs (used as node_annotation here).
id <- data.frame(
  ID = names(sort(net$colors))         # node IDs in module order
)

# 7. Assemble the tbl_graph.
#    Uses the sparsified TOM as edge weights and the WGCNA module colours as
#    the Modularity assignment (no community detection is recomputed).
obj <- build_graph_from_wgcna(
  wgcna_tom       = trans_TOM,  # sparsified TOM (from trans_TOM_in_WGCNA)
  module          = module,     # data.frame with ID + Module columns
  node_annotation = id          # optional per-node metadata
)
obj
\end{verbatim}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Build graph from multi-graph}\label{build-graph-from-multi-graph}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Consensus{-}network workflow {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# (1) Build several per{-}method networks from the SAME matrix.}
\CommentTok{\# (2) Convert each tbl\_graph back to an adjacency matrix.}
\CommentTok{\# (3) Fuse the per{-}method adjacencies into ONE consensus tbl\_graph.}

\CommentTok{\# Restrict to the 40 most abundant OTUs to keep this chunk responsive.}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)}
\NormalTok{mat }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{as.matrix}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)}
\NormalTok{mat }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ mat[}\FunctionTok{order}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{rowSums}\NormalTok{(mat), }\AttributeTok{decreasing =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)[}\FunctionTok{seq\_len}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{40}\NormalTok{)], ]}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} (1) Three per{-}method networks on the same data {-}{-}{-}{-}}

\CommentTok{\# Spearman correlation through psych::corr.test — baseline method.}
\NormalTok{g\_cor   }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{  mat,                              }\CommentTok{\# input matrix (variables x samples)}
  \AttributeTok{method        =} \StringTok{"cor"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: psych::corr.test}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method    =} \StringTok{"spearman"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# Spearman correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc          =} \StringTok{"BH"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction (Benjamini{-}Hochberg)}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.4}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# adjusted p{-}value cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{module.method =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,    }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{seed          =} \DecValTok{1}                 \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 5 we use the 5  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# WGCNA{-}style correlation (WGCNA::corAndPvalue + the package\textquotesingle{}s thresholding).}
\NormalTok{g\_wgcna }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{  mat,                              }\CommentTok{\# input matrix (variables x samples)}
  \AttributeTok{method        =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: WGCNA::corAndPvalue}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method    =} \StringTok{"spearman"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# Spearman correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc          =} \StringTok{"BH"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction (Benjamini{-}Hochberg)}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.4}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# adjusted p{-}value cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{module.method =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,    }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{seed          =} \DecValTok{1}                 \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 6 we use the 6  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Hmisc::rcorr — another correlation engine; slightly different NA/tie behaviour.}
\NormalTok{g\_hmisc }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{  mat,                              }\CommentTok{\# input matrix (variables x samples)}
  \AttributeTok{method        =} \StringTok{"Hmisc"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: Hmisc::rcorr}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method    =} \StringTok{"spearman"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# Spearman correlation}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.4}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold   =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# raw p{-}value cutoff (no \textasciigrave{}proc\textasciigrave{} here)}
  \AttributeTok{module.method =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,    }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{seed          =} \DecValTok{1}                 \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 4 we use the 4  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} (2) tbl\_graph {-}\textgreater{} adjacency matrix {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}get\_graph\_adjacency()\textasciigrave{} recovers the weighted adjacency matrix from a tbl\_graph,}
\CommentTok{\# which is the format expected by build\_graph\_from\_consensus().}
\NormalTok{adj\_cor   }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_graph\_adjacency}\NormalTok{(g\_cor)}
\NormalTok{adj\_wgcna }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_graph\_adjacency}\NormalTok{(g\_wgcna)}
\NormalTok{adj\_hmisc }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_graph\_adjacency}\NormalTok{(g\_hmisc)}

\CommentTok{\# Bundle per{-}method matrices into a NAMED list.}
\CommentTok{\# Names ("cor", "wgcna", "hmisc") are kept as method labels downstream.}
\NormalTok{adj\_list }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{list}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{cor =}\NormalTok{ adj\_cor, }\AttributeTok{wgcna =}\NormalTok{ adj\_wgcna, }\AttributeTok{hmisc =}\NormalTok{ adj\_hmisc)}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} (3) Consensus fusion {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Borda rank fusion: ranks every pair by |w| in each method, averages the ranks,}
\CommentTok{\# then min{-}max rescales to [{-}1, 1]. Robust to per{-}method weight{-}scale differences.}
\NormalTok{g\_borda }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_consensus}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{adj\_list              =}\NormalTok{ adj\_list,        }\CommentTok{\# named list of per{-}method adjacency matrices}
  \AttributeTok{method                =} \StringTok{"rank\_fusion"}\NormalTok{,   }\CommentTok{\# fusion strategy}
  \AttributeTok{rank\_fusion\_algorithm =} \StringTok{"borda"}\NormalTok{,         }\CommentTok{\# specific rank{-}fusion variant}
  \AttributeTok{threshold             =} \FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# final |w| cutoff applied to the fused matrix}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules           =} \DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}5 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed                  =} \DecValTok{1}                \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## The max module in network is 3 we use the 3  modules for next analysis
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{g\_borda}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\chapter{Random Matrix Theory (RMT)--based random network}\label{random-matrix-theory-rmtbased-random-network}

\begin{quote}
When a numerical matrix is used as input, it is necessary to filter and screen the resulting correlation matrix. Empirical thresholds are commonly adopted, usually between 0.6 to 0.85; however, these thresholds are largely subjective.
\end{quote}

\begin{quote}
Compared with empirical thresholding, random matrix theory (RMT) provides a data-driven and objective approach for determining correlation thresholds, effectively separating true biological signals from random noise and improving the robustness and comparability of ecological networks.
\end{quote}

\section{Random Matrix Theory (RMT)}\label{random-matrix-theory-rmt}

\begin{quote}
Load R Package
\end{quote}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggNetView)   }\CommentTok{\# graph builders + info{-}extraction helpers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Compute RMT threshold}\label{compute-rmt-threshold}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} RMT threshold scan \#1: WGCNA correlation backend {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}ggNetView\_RMT()\textasciigrave{} scans a series of candidate |r| thresholds, builds the}
\CommentTok{\# thresholded correlation matrix at each, computes its eigenvalue spacing}
\CommentTok{\# distribution (NNSD), and picks the threshold at which the NNSD transitions}
\CommentTok{\# from Gaussian Orthogonal Ensemble (signal + noise) to Poisson (noise{-}free,}
\CommentTok{\# modular network) — this is the RMT{-}recommended cutoff.}
\NormalTok{out }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView\_RMT}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative, }\CommentTok{\# input matrix (variables x samples)}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# already relative{-}abundance, no extra transform}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,           }\CommentTok{\# use WGCNA::corAndPvalue for correlation}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,         }\CommentTok{\# Pearson correlation}
  \AttributeTok{unfold.method    =} \StringTok{"gaussian"}\NormalTok{,        }\CommentTok{\# Gaussian KDE unfolding of eigenvalues}
  \AttributeTok{bandwidth        =} \StringTok{"nrd0"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# KDE bandwidth rule (stats::density default)}
  \AttributeTok{nr.fit.points    =} \DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# support points (only used if unfold.method = "spline")}
  \AttributeTok{discard.outliers =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# IQR{-}trim extreme eigenvalues before unfolding}
  \AttributeTok{discard.zeros    =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# drop all{-}zero rows/cols after thresholding}
  \AttributeTok{min.mat.dim      =} \DecValTok{40}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# early{-}stop if effective dim drops below 40}
  \AttributeTok{max.ev.spacing   =} \DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{,                 }\CommentTok{\# truncate NNSD tail at spacing = 3}
  \AttributeTok{save\_plots       =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# don\textquotesingle{}t write diagnostic PNGs to disk}
  \AttributeTok{out\_dir          =} \StringTok{"RMT\_plots"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# (only used when save\_plots = TRUE)}
  \AttributeTok{verbose          =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# print scan progress to console}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} RMT threshold scan \#2: plain \textasciigrave{}cor()\textasciigrave{} backend for comparison {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Same parameters as \textasciigrave{}out\textasciigrave{} above, but with \textasciigrave{}method = "cor"\textasciigrave{} (base stats::cor /}
\CommentTok{\# psych::corr.test) instead of WGCNA. Useful as a sanity check — the chosen}
\CommentTok{\# threshold should be very close across the two backends}
\NormalTok{out2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView\_RMT}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"cor"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# \textless{}{-}{-} only difference vs. \textasciigrave{}out\textasciigrave{}}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{unfold.method    =} \StringTok{"gaussian"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{bandwidth        =} \StringTok{"nrd0"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{nr.fit.points    =} \DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{discard.outliers =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{discard.zeros    =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{min.mat.dim      =} \DecValTok{40}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{max.ev.spacing   =} \DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{save\_plots       =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{out\_dir          =} \StringTok{"RMT\_plots"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{verbose          =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Build graph from matrix based on RMT-threshold}\label{build-graph-from-matrix-based-on-rmt-threshold}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Construct the tidygraph network using the RMT{-}selected r threshold {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Replaces the usual subjective r{-}cutoff (e.g. 0.6–0.85) with the data{-}driven}
\CommentTok{\# value \textasciigrave{}out$chosen\_threshold\textasciigrave{} returned above.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,      }\CommentTok{\# variable x sample matrix}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,                 }\CommentTok{\# match the transform used for RMT scan}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =}\NormalTok{ out}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{chosen\_threshold,   }\CommentTok{\# RMT{-}derived |r| cutoff (objective)}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,                   }\CommentTok{\# significance cutoff on adjusted p{-}values}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend (must match RMT scan)}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# correlation type}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"bonferroni"}\NormalTok{,           }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction for p{-}values}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,                }\CommentTok{\# taxonomy joined onto each node (by name)}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                     }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; smaller ones {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                    \CommentTok{\# reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}


\CommentTok{\# Print to inspect node/edge counts and the columns now available on nodes}
\CommentTok{\# (modularity, modularity2, modularity3, Modularity, Degree, Strength + taxonomy)}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tbl_graph: 213 nodes and 844 edges
## #
## # An undirected simple graph with 29 components
## #
## # Node Data: 213 x 14 (active)
##    name    modularity modularity2 modularity3 Modularity Degree Strength Kingdom
##    <chr>   <fct>      <ord>       <chr>       <ord>       <dbl>    <dbl> <chr>  
##  1 ASV_649 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  2 ASV_705 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  3 ASV_12~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  4 ASV_13~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  5 ASV_14~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  6 ASV_14~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  7 ASV_24~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
##  8 ASV_25~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.4 Bacter~
##  9 ASV_28~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
## 10 ASV_28~ 5          5           5           5              27     26.5 Bacter~
## # i 203 more rows
## # i 6 more variables: Phylum <chr>, Class <chr>, Order <chr>, Family <chr>,
## #   Genus <chr>, Species <chr>
## #
## # Edge Data: 844 x 5
##    from    to weight correlation corr_direction
##   <int> <int>  <dbl>       <dbl> <chr>         
## 1   194   195  0.959       0.959 Positive      
## 2   185   208  0.954       0.954 Positive      
## 3   185   213  0.957       0.957 Positive      
## # i 841 more rows
\end{verbatim}

\chapter{Get network information}\label{get-network-information}

\begin{quote}
Once the graph object is constructed, basic network information can be retrieved, and subnetworks can be extracted.
\end{quote}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\begin{quote}
Load R Package
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(tidyverse)   }\CommentTok{\# data manipulation / piping utilities}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggNetView)   }\CommentTok{\# graph builders + info{-}extraction helpers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Built{-}in example datasets {-}{-}{-}{-}}

\CommentTok{\# Relative abundance table of rarefied ASVs/OTUs (rows = ASVs, cols = samples).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare\_relative"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859   18
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{otu\_rare\_relative[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##              KO1        KO2        KO3        KO4        KO5
## ASV_1 0.03306667 0.05453333 0.02013333 0.03613333 0.02686667
## ASV_2 0.05750000 0.03393333 0.06046667 0.05810000 0.07320000
## ASV_3 0.01733333 0.01296667 0.02290000 0.02336667 0.03106667
## ASV_4 0.04266667 0.01093333 0.01416667 0.01933333 0.03346667
## ASV_6 0.02646667 0.01856667 0.02110000 0.02353333 0.03806667
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Taxonomic annotation; first column must be the ASV/OTU ID.}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"tax\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(tax\_tab)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859    8
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{tax\_tab[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tibble: 5 x 5
##   OTUID  Kingdom  Phylum          Class          Order            
##   <chr>  <chr>    <chr>           <chr>          <chr>            
## 1 ASV_2  Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 2 ASV_3  Bacteria Verrucomicrobia Spartobacteria Unassigned       
## 3 ASV_31 Bacteria Actinobacteria  Actinobacteria Actinomycetales  
## 4 ASV_27 Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 5 ASV_9  Bacteria Unassigned      Unassigned     Unassigned
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build a co{-}occurrence network as the input for downstream queries {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,  }\CommentTok{\# variables x samples numeric matrix}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# input already relative abundance, no extra transform}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff for keeping an edge}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# adjusted p{-}value cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: WGCNA::corAndPvalue}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# Pearson correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"bonferroni"}\NormalTok{,       }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,            }\CommentTok{\# taxonomy joined onto each node by name}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                 }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Split the graph by \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} (one subgraph per module) {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}select\_module = NULL\textasciigrave{} {-}\textgreater{} no merged subgraph; only per{-}module split + stats.}
\NormalTok{subgraph\_info }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_subgraph}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,   }\CommentTok{\# input tbl\_graph (must have a \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} column on nodes)}
  \AttributeTok{select\_module =} \ConstantTok{NULL}         \CommentTok{\# NULL {-}\textgreater{} don\textquotesingle{}t build a merged subgraph}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{select\_module =} \StringTok{"5"}          \CommentTok{\# character vector of module labels to retain}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

\section{Sub-network (sample) information}\label{sub-network-sample-information}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Extract sample{-}level subgraphs {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# For each sample (column of \textasciigrave{}mat\textasciigrave{}), an OTU is treated as "present" when}
\CommentTok{\# its abundance is \textgreater{} \textasciigrave{}min\_abundance\textasciigrave{} (default 0). The induced subgraph of}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}graph\_obj\textasciigrave{} on those present OTUs is returned per sample. With}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}select\_sample\textasciigrave{} + \textasciigrave{}combine\textasciigrave{}, a single merged subgraph is also returned.}
\NormalTok{res }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_sample\_subgraph}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,                        }\CommentTok{\# the network built above}
  \AttributeTok{mat           =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,                }\CommentTok{\# same matrix used to judge "presence" (rownames must match node names)}
  \AttributeTok{select\_sample =} \FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{], }\CommentTok{\# sample IDs to merge into one subgraph (here: first 3 samples)}
  \AttributeTok{combine       =} \StringTok{"union"}                           \CommentTok{\# "union" = OTUs present in ANY selected sample; "intersect" = OTUs present in ALL}
\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{names}\NormalTok{(res)   }\CommentTok{\# "sub\_graph\_all", "stat\_sample", "sub\_graph\_select"}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] "sub_graph_all"    "stat_sample"      "sub_graph_select"
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Inspect the merged 3{-}sample subgraph {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{sub\_graph\_select   }\CommentTok{\# tbl\_graph: nodes = union/intersect of present OTUs across the 3 samples}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\chapter{Extract subgraph}\label{extract-subgraph}

\begin{quote}
Load R Package
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(tidyverse)   }\CommentTok{\# data manipulation / piping utilities}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggNetView)   }\CommentTok{\# graph builders + info{-}extraction helpers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Access built{-}in example datasets in ggNetView}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Dataset 1: Relative abundance table of rarefied ASVs/OTUs {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Rows = ASVs/OTUs (features / variables), Columns = samples}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare\_relative"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)  }\CommentTok{\# Check matrix dimensions: n\_features x n\_samples }
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] 2859   18
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{otu\_rare\_relative[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{] }\CommentTok{\# Preview first 5 rows x 5 cols to confirm data layout}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Dataset 2: Taxonomic annotation table for ASVs/OTUs {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Rows = ASVs/OTUs (row names should match the abundance table above)}
\CommentTok{\# Columns = taxonomic ranks (Kingdom \textasciitilde{} Species, etc.)}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"tax\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(tax\_tab)}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tibble: 5 x 5
##   OTUID  Kingdom  Phylum          Class          Order            
##   <chr>  <chr>    <chr>           <chr>          <chr>            
## 1 ASV_2  Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 2 ASV_3  Bacteria Verrucomicrobia Spartobacteria Unassigned       
## 3 ASV_31 Bacteria Actinobacteria  Actinobacteria Actinomycetales  
## 4 ASV_27 Archaea  Thaumarchaeota  Unassigned     Nitrososphaerales
## 5 ASV_9  Bacteria Unassigned      Unassigned     Unassigned
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Build a correlation{-}based graph object (tbl\_graph) from the abundance matrix.}
\CommentTok{\# This graph object is the foundation for downstream subgraph extraction,}
\CommentTok{\# layout computation, and visualization.}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative, }\CommentTok{\# Input matrix: features (rows) x samples (cols)}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# Data transformation before correlation;  Other options: "scale" / "center" / "log2" / "log10" / "ln" / "rrarefy" / "rrarefy\_relative"}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# Correlation cutoff: keep edges only if |r| \textgreater{}= 0.7}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# Significance cutoff: keep edges only if p \textless{} 0.05}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,           }\CommentTok{\# Association method: Other options: "SpiecEasi" / "SPARCC" / "cor" / "Hmisc"}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,         }\CommentTok{\# Correlation type: Pearson (also: "spearman" / "kendall")}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"bonferroni"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# Multiple{-}testing correction for p{-}values.}
                                        \CommentTok{\# Other options: "BH" / "fdr" / "holm" / "hochberg" / ...}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# Community / module detection algorithm.  Other options: "Walktrap" / "Edge\_betweenness" / "Spinglass"}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,           }\CommentTok{\# Node metadata (taxonomy) attached to each node}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# Keep only the top 15 largest modules for downstream use}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# Random seed for reproducible module detection}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj                               }\CommentTok{\# Print the graph object: node/edge counts and attributes}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Extract subgraph by module}\label{extract-subgraph-by-module}

\subsection{select subgraph by modularity}\label{select-subgraph-by-modularity}

\begin{quote}
Select one modularity
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Extract the subgraph corresponding to module "5".}
\CommentTok{\# Internally, get\_subgraph():}
\CommentTok{\#   1) splits all nodes by the \textasciigrave{}Modularity\textasciigrave{} attribute,}
\CommentTok{\#   2) builds one subgraph per module and stores them in \textasciigrave{}sub\_graph\_all\textasciigrave{},}
\CommentTok{\#   3) if \textasciigrave{}select\_module\textasciigrave{} is provided, also returns \textasciigrave{}sub\_graph\_select\textasciigrave{}}
\CommentTok{\#      (the graph filtered to those modules via tidygraph::filter),}
\CommentTok{\#   4) returns \textasciigrave{}stat\_module\textasciigrave{}: a table of node counts per module across the full graph.}
\NormalTok{module5\_subgraph\_info }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_subgraph}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{graph\_obj =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
                                      \AttributeTok{select\_module =} \StringTok{"5"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Extract subgraph by sample}\label{extract-subgraph-by-sample}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Load example datasets shipped with ggNetView {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Relative{-}abundance table of rarefied ASVs/OTUs (rows = features, cols = samples)}

\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare\_relative"}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# Taxonomic annotation table for those ASVs/OTUs (used as node metadata)}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"tax\_tab"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build the correlation{-}based network {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# This produces a tbl\_graph that downstream subgraph / layout / plotting}
\CommentTok{\# functions all consume.}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative, }\CommentTok{\# Feature{-}by{-}sample matrix}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# No transform: input is already relative abundance}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# Keep edges with |r| \textgreater{}= 0.7}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,              }\CommentTok{\# Keep edges with p \textless{} 0.05}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,           }\CommentTok{\# WGCNA correlation + p{-}values}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,         }\CommentTok{\# Pearson correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"bonferroni"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# Bonferroni multiple{-}testing correction}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# Community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,           }\CommentTok{\# Attach taxonomy to each node}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# Keep the 15 largest modules}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}               \CommentTok{\# Reproducible module assignment}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{select subgraph by sample name}\label{select-subgraph-by-sample-name}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Extract sample{-}level subgraphs {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# For each sample (column of \textasciigrave{}mat\textasciigrave{}), an OTU is treated as "present" when}
\CommentTok{\# its abundance is \textgreater{} \textasciigrave{}min\_abundance\textasciigrave{} (default 0). The induced subgraph of}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}graph\_obj\textasciigrave{} on those present OTUs is returned per sample. With}
\CommentTok{\# \textasciigrave{}select\_sample\textasciigrave{} + \textasciigrave{}combine\textasciigrave{}, a single merged subgraph is also returned.}
\NormalTok{res }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{get\_sample\_subgraph}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,                       }\CommentTok{\# The network built above}
  \AttributeTok{mat           =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,               }\CommentTok{\# Same matrix used to judge "presence"}
  \AttributeTok{select\_sample =} \FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)[}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{],}\CommentTok{\# Merge the first 3 samples}
  \AttributeTok{combine       =} \StringTok{"union"}                          \CommentTok{\# Keep OTUs present in ANY of the 3 samples (use "intersect" for OTUs present in ALL)}
\NormalTok{)}

\FunctionTok{class}\NormalTok{(res)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] "list"
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{names}\NormalTok{(res)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] "sub_graph_all"    "stat_sample"      "sub_graph_select"
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Inspect outputs {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Per{-}sample summary table: Sample / Node / Edge / Status.}
\CommentTok{\# Includes every sample in \textasciigrave{}mat\textasciigrave{}, even those whose subgraph is empty.}
\NormalTok{res}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{stat\_sample}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\chapter{Network layout}\label{network-layout}

\begin{quote}
Load R Package
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(tidyverse)   }\CommentTok{\# data manipulation / piping utilities}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggNetView)   }\CommentTok{\# graph builders + layout/plotting frontend}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Example data
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Built{-}in example datasets {-}{-}{-}{-}}

\CommentTok{\# Relative abundance table of rarefied ASVs/OTUs (rows = ASVs, cols = samples).}
\FunctionTok{data}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"otu\_rare\_relative"}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{dim}\NormalTok{(otu\_rare\_relative)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
Build graph object
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-} Build a co{-}occurrence network used by every layout demo below {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{graph\_obj }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{build\_graph\_from\_mat}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{mat              =}\NormalTok{ otu\_rare\_relative,  }\CommentTok{\# variables x samples numeric matrix}
  \AttributeTok{transfrom.method =} \StringTok{"none"}\NormalTok{,             }\CommentTok{\# already relative abundance, no extra transform}
  \AttributeTok{r.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{,                }\CommentTok{\# |r| cutoff for keeping an edge}
  \AttributeTok{p.threshold      =} \FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# adjusted p{-}value cutoff}
  \AttributeTok{method           =} \StringTok{"WGCNA"}\NormalTok{,            }\CommentTok{\# correlation backend: WGCNA::corAndPvalue}
  \AttributeTok{cor.method       =} \StringTok{"pearson"}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# Pearson correlation}
  \AttributeTok{proc             =} \StringTok{"BH"}\NormalTok{,               }\CommentTok{\# multiple{-}testing correction (Benjamini{-}Hochberg)}
  \AttributeTok{module.method    =} \StringTok{"Fast\_greedy"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# community detection algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{node\_annotation  =}\NormalTok{ tax\_tab,            }\CommentTok{\# taxonomy joined onto nodes by name}
  \AttributeTok{top\_modules      =} \DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{,                 }\CommentTok{\# keep top{-}15 modules; rest {-}\textgreater{} "Others"}
  \AttributeTok{seed             =} \DecValTok{1115}                \CommentTok{\# fix RNG for reproducibility}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{graph\_obj}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}

\section{Full graph layout}\label{full-graph-layout}

\subsection{Gephi layout}\label{gephi-layout}

\begin{quote}
layout.module = ``random''
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Gephi{-}style ForceAtlas2{-}like layout: good general{-}purpose layout for}
\CommentTok{\# scale{-}free / community{-}rich biological networks.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,    }\CommentTok{\# the tbl\_graph to draw}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"gephi"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# global node{-}positioning algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,            }\CommentTok{\# don\textquotesingle{}t pull all modules toward the plot center}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# scale each module inward (smaller = tighter modules)}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"random"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# how modules are placed relative to each other: "random" / "adjacent" / "order"}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{, }\CommentTok{\# grouping variable used for per{-}module layout}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}  \CommentTok{\# node fill colour mapped to module ID}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-49-1} \end{center}

\begin{quote}
layout.module = ``adjacent''
\end{quote}

\begin{Highlighting}[]

\CommentTok{\# scale{-}free / community{-}rich biological networks.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,    }\CommentTok{\# the tbl\_graph to draw}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"gephi"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# global node{-}positioning algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,            }\CommentTok{\# don\textquotesingle{}t pull all modules toward the plot center}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# scale each module inward (smaller = tighter modules)}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# how modules are placed relative to each other: "random" / "adjacent" / "order"}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{, }\CommentTok{\# grouping variable used for per{-}module layout}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}  \CommentTok{\# node fill colour mapped to module ID}
\NormalTok{)}

\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Gephi{-}style ForceAtlas2{-}like layout: good general{-}purpose layout for}
\CommentTok{\# scale{-}free / community{-}rich biological networks.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,    }\CommentTok{\# the tbl\_graph to draw}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"gephi"}\NormalTok{,      }\CommentTok{\# global node{-}positioning algorithm}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,            }\CommentTok{\# don\textquotesingle{}t pull all modules toward the plot center}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,          }\CommentTok{\# scale each module inward (smaller = tighter modules)}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"order"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# how modules are placed relative to each other: "random" / "adjacent" / "order"}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{, }\CommentTok{\# grouping variable used for per{-}module layout}

\subsection{Fruchterman--Reingold force-directed layout}\label{fruchtermanreingold-force-directed-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# FR2: a force{-}directed layout (springs pull connected nodes, repulsion pushes}
\CommentTok{\# all others apart). Classic choice; reveals dense vs. sparse regions clearly.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"fr2"}\NormalTok{,        }\CommentTok{\# Fruchterman{-}Reingold variant}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"random"}\NormalTok{,     }\CommentTok{\# modules placed independently from each other}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-52-1} \end{center}

\subsection{Diamond layout}\label{diamond-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "diamond": modules arranged on a rhombic / diamond{-}shaped lattice.}
\CommentTok{\# Good for showing many modules side{-}by{-}side without overlap.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"diamond"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,   }\CommentTok{\# neighbouring modules placed close together to minimise gaps}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-53-1} \end{center}

\subsection{KK layout}\label{kk-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Kamada{-}Kawai force{-}directed layout: like FR but optimises a stress function;}
\CommentTok{\# tends to give smoother, more even spacing for medium{-}sized networks.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"kk"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-54-1} \end{center}

\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"nicely"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Petal layout}\label{petal-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "petal": each module is laid out as a circular petal radiating from the}

\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}

\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# "circle": all nodes placed on a single ring. Useful for small networks or for}

\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}

\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{)}

\subsection{Square layout}\label{square-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "square": like rectangle but constrained to a square aspect ratio.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"square"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-64-1} \end{center}

\subsection{Star layout}\label{star-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "star": one central node + others arranged as rays radiating outward.}
\CommentTok{\# Best when there is a clear hub node; less useful for community{-}rich graphs.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"star"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-65-1} \end{center}

\subsection{Star\_concentric layout}\label{star_concentric-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "star\_concentric": star layout with multiple concentric rings — nodes ranked}
\CommentTok{\# by some centrality measure end up closer to the centre.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"star\_concentric"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{p}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{center}\includegraphics{ggNetView-manual_files/figure-latex/unnamed-chunk-66-1} \end{center}

\subsection{Stress layout}\label{stress-layout}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# "stress": stress{-}majorisation layout (from graphlayouts). Generally produces}
\CommentTok{\# the cleanest "FR/KK{-}like" results and is reproducible across runs — a strong}
\CommentTok{\# default for publication figures.}
\NormalTok{p }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ggNetView}\NormalTok{(}
  \AttributeTok{graph\_obj     =}\NormalTok{ graph\_obj,}
  \AttributeTok{layout        =} \StringTok{"stress"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{center        =}\NormalTok{ F,}
  \AttributeTok{shrink        =} \FloatTok{0.8}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{layout.module =} \StringTok{"adjacent"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{group.by      =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}\NormalTok{,}
  \AttributeTok{fill.by       =} \StringTok{"Modularity"}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
